Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We need to find the number of subsequences of length 3 that form a geometric progression with common ratio k
2. A geometric progression with ratio k has the form: b, b*k, b*k²
3. We need to count triplets (i, j, m) where i < j < m such that a[i] * k = a[j] and a[j] * k = a[m]
4. This means: a[i] * k² = a[m] and a[i] * k = a[j]

## Approach

The key insight is that for any valid triplet (i, j, m), we need:
- a[j] = a[i] * k
- a[m] = a[j] * k = a[i] * k²

So for each position j, we can:
1. Count how many elements before j have value a[j] / k
2. Count how many elements after j have value a[j] * k

Then the contribution of position j to the total count is (count_before[j]) * (count_after[j])

## Step-by-step Solution

1. **Handle special case**: If k = 1, then we need to count triplets where all three elements are equal
2. **For k ≠ 1**: For each middle element, count valid left and right elements
3. **Optimization**: Use prefix and suffix counts to avoid O(n²) complexity

## Implementation Strategy

1. Create a frequency map for the array
2. For each element, calculate how many valid pairs can be formed
3. For each index, check if a[j] is divisible by k (for k > 1)
4. Count valid left elements (a[i] such that a[i] * k = a[j])
5. Count valid right elements (a[m] such that a[j] * k = a[m])

```python
n, k = map(int, input().split())
a = list(map(int, input().split()))

# Handle the special case when k = 1
# In this case, we need triplets of equal elements
if k == 1:
    # Count frequency of each element
    freq = {}
    for num in a:
        freq[num] = freq.get(num, 0) + 1
    
    total = 0
    for count in freq.values():
        if count >= 3:
            total += count * (count - 1) * (count - 2) // 6
    print(total)
else:
    # For k != 1
    total = 0
    
    # Count frequency of each element in the array
    # We'll use prefix and suffix counts
    prefix_count = {}
    suffix_count = {}
    
    # Initialize suffix_count with all elements
    for num in a:
        suffix_count[num] = suffix_count.get(num, 0) + 1
    
    # Process each element as the middle element of the triplet
    for i in range(n):
        current = a[i]
        
        # For the middle element to be current, we need:
        # left element: current / k (must be integer)
        # right element: current * k
        
        # Check if current is divisible by k
        if current % k == 0:
            left_val = current // k
            right_val = current * k
            
            # Add count of left elements before index i
            left_count = prefix_count.get(left_val, 0)
            
            # Add count of right elements after index i
            right_count = suffix_count.get(right_val, 0)
            
            total += left_count * right_count
        
        # Update prefix_count for current element
        prefix_count[current] = prefix_count.get(current, 0) + 1
        
        # Update suffix_count for current element (we remove it after processing)
        suffix_count[current] -= 1
        if suffix_count[current] == 0:
            del suffix_count[current]
    
    print(total)
```

Wait, I need to reconsider the suffix update. Let me trace through the first example:

n=5, k=2, array=[1,1,2,2,4]

For each position as middle:
- i=0: current=1, 1%2≠0, skip
- i=1: current=1, 1%2≠0, skip  
- i=2: current=2, 2%2=0, left_val=1, right_val=4
  - prefix_count: {1:2}, suffix_count: {1:0,2:1,4:1}
  - left_count=2, right_count=1, add 2*1=2
- i=3: current=2, 2%2=0, left_val=1, right_val=4
  - prefix_count: {1:2,2:1}, suffix_count: {2:0,4:1}
  - left_count=2, right_count=1, add 2*1=2
- i=4: current=4, 4%2=0, left_val=2, right_val=8
  - prefix_count: {1:2,2:2}, suffix_count: {4:0}
  - left_count=2, right_count=0, add 0

Total = 4, which matches the expected output!

```python
n, k = map(int, input().split())
a = list(map(int, input().split()))

if k == 1:
    # Count frequency of each element
    freq = {}
    for num in a:
        freq[num] = freq.get(num, 0) + 1
    
    total = 0
    for count in freq.values():
        if count >= 3:
            total += count * (count - 1) * (count - 2) // 6
    print(total)
else:
    total = 0
    
    # Use prefix and suffix counts
    prefix_count = {}
    suffix_count = {}
    
    # Initialize suffix_count with all elements
    for num in a:
        suffix_count[num] = suffix_count.get(num, 0) + 1
    
    # Process each position as the middle element
    for i in range(n):
        current = a[i]
        
        # Check if current is divisible by k
        if current % k == 0:
            left_val = current // k
            right_val = current * k
            
            # Count elements before i with value left_val
            left_count = prefix_count.get(left_val, 0)
            
            # Count elements after i with value right_val
            right_count = suffix_count.get(right_val, 0)
            
            total += left_count * right_count
        
        # Update prefix_count for current element
        prefix_count[current] = prefix_count.get(current, 0) + 1
        
        # Update suffix_count for current element (remove it from suffix)
        suffix_count[current] = suffix_count.get(current, 0) - 1
        if suffix_count[current] == 0:
            del suffix_count[current]
    
    print(total)
```

A geometric progression with common ratio k is a sequence of numbers of the form b·k0, b·k1, ..., b·kr - 1.

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We need to find the number of subsequences of length 3 that form a geometric progression with common ratio k
2. A geometric progression with ratio k has the form: b, b*k, b*k²
3. We need to count triplets (i, j, m) where i < j < m and a[i], a[j], a[m] form a geometric progression with ratio k

## Key Insight

For three elements to form a geometric progression with ratio k:
- If we have elements at positions i, j, m such that i < j < m
- Then a[j] must be a[i] * k and a[m] must be a[i] * k²
- Or equivalently: a[j] = a[i] * k and a[m] = a[j] * k = a[i] * k²

## Approach

1. For each possible middle element (at position j), we need to count:
   - How many elements before j have value equal to a[j] / k
   - How many elements after j have value equal to a[j] * k

2. For each middle element, the total contribution is: (count_before) × (count_after)

3. We can precompute the frequency of elements to efficiently calculate these counts.

## Step-by-step Solution

1. Read input
2. For each position j, check if a[j] is divisible by k (to maintain integer values)
3. If divisible, calculate the required first element (a[j]/k) and third element (a[j]*k)
4. Count how many elements before j equal to first element
5. Count how many elements after j equal to third element
6. Add the product to our answer

## Special Cases

- When k = 1: the progression is b, b, b (all elements equal)
- When k > 1: we need to handle division carefully

```python
n, k = map(int, input().split())
a = list(map(int, input().split()))

count = 0

# For each middle position j
for j in range(1, n - 1):
    # Check if we can form a geometric progression with ratio k
    # The middle element is a[j]
    # First element should be a[j] / k
    # Third element should be a[j] * k
    
    # If k == 1, all elements must be equal
    if k == 1:
        # We need three equal elements
        # For each j, we count elements before and after with same value
        first_val = a[j]
        before_count = 0
        after_count = 0
        
        for i in range(j):
            if a[i] == first_val:
                before_count += 1
                
        for i in range(j + 1, n):
            if a[i] == first_val:
                after_count += 1
                
        count += before_count * after_count
    else:
        # For k != 1
        # First element should be a[j] / k
        # Third element should be a[j] * k
        
        # Check if a[j] is divisible by k
        if a[j] % k == 0:
            first_val = a[j] // k
            third_val = a[j] * k
            
            before_count = 0
            for i in range(j):
                if a[i] == first_val:
                    before_count += 1
                    
            after_count = 0
            for i in range(j + 1, n):
                if a[i] == third_val:
                    after_count += 1
                    
            count += before_count * after_count

print(count)
```

Actually, for the given constraints (n ≤ 2×10⁵), the O(n²) solution might be acceptable, but let me try to optimize it further.

```python
n, k = map(int, input().split())
a = list(map(int, input().split()))

count = 0

# For each middle position j
for j in range(1, n - 1):
    if k == 1:
        # All elements must be equal
        target = a[j]
        before_count = sum(1 for i in range(j) if a[i] == target)
        after_count = sum(1 for i in range(j + 1, n) if a[i] == target)
        count += before_count * after_count
    else:
        # Check divisibility
        if a[j] % k == 0:
            first_val = a[j] // k
            third_val = a[j] * k
            
            before_count = 0
            for i in range(j):
                if a[i] == first_val:
                    before_count += 1
                    
            after_count = 0
            for i in range(j + 1, n):
                if a[i] == third_val:
                    after_count += 1
                    
            count += before_count * after_count

print(count)
```

a = list(map(int, input().split()))

count = 0

# For each middle position j
for j in range(1, n - 1):
    if k == 1:
        # All elements must be equal
        target = a[j]
        before_count = sum(1 for i in range(j) if a[i] == target)
        after_count = sum(1 for i in range(j + 1, n) if a[i] == target)
        count += before_count * after_count
    else:
        # Check divisibility
        if a[j] % k == 0:
            first_val = a[j] // k
            third_val = a[j] * k
            
            before_count = 0
            for i in range(j):
                if a[i] == first_val:
                    before_count += 1
                    
            after_count = 0
            for i in range(j + 1, n):
                if a[i] == third_val:
                    after_count += 1
                    
            count += before_count * after_count

print(count)

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

We need to find the number of subsequences of length 3 that form a geometric progression with common ratio k.

A geometric progression with ratio k has the form: b, b×k, b×k².

So we're looking for triples (i, j, l) where i < j < l and a[i] = b, a[j] = b×k, a[l] = b×k².

## Key Insights

1. For each possible middle element (at position j), we need to count:
   - How many elements before it equal b (first term)
   - How many elements after it equal b×k² (third term)

2. The middle element must be b×k, where b is the first term.

3. We can iterate through each position as the middle element of our triple.

## Algorithm Steps

1. For each position j (from 1 to n-2, 0-indexed):
   - Check if a[j] is divisible by k (to be b×k)
   - If yes, compute b = a[j] / k
   - Count how many elements before j equal b
   - Count how many elements after j equal b×k²
   - Add the product of these counts to our answer

2. Handle special cases:
   - When k = 1: all elements must be equal, so we need to count triplets of equal elements
   - When k > 1 or k < 1: need to handle division carefully

## Implementation Details

- Use prefix counts to efficiently count elements before current position
- Use suffix counts to efficiently count elements after current position
- For division: we need to ensure that a[j] is divisible by k and that the result makes sense

```python
n, k = map(int, input().split())
a = list(map(int, input().split()))

if k == 1:
    # When k = 1, we need triplets of equal elements
    # Count frequency of each value
    from collections import Counter
    count = Counter(a)
    result = 0
    for val, freq in count.items():
        if freq >= 3:
            result += freq * (freq - 1) * (freq - 2) // 6
    print(result)
else:
    # For k != 1, we need to find geometric progressions of form b, b*k, b*k*k
    result = 0
    
    # Precompute suffix counts - how many times each value appears from position j+1 to end
    suffix_count = {}
    # Initialize with last element
    suffix_count[a[-1]] = 1
    
    # Process from right to left to build suffix counts
    for i in range(n-2, -1, -1):
        suffix_count[a[i]] = suffix_count.get(a[i], 0) + 1
    
    # Process each position as the middle element
    prefix_count = {}
    
    for j in range(n-1):  # j is the middle element index
        # Check if a[j] can be b*k
        if a[j] % k == 0:
            b = a[j] // k
            # The first term should be b, middle term is b*k, third term should be b*k*k
            third_term = b * k * k
            
            # Count how many times b appears before position j
            first_count = prefix_count.get(b, 0)
            
            # Count how many times third_term appears after position j
            third_count = suffix_count.get(third_term, 0)
            
            result += first_count * third_count
        
        # Add current element to prefix_count for future positions
        prefix_count[a[j]] = prefix_count.get(a[j], 0) + 1
        
        # Update suffix_count for the next iteration (we're moving left)
        # We already built suffix_count from right to left, so we need to remove current element
        # Actually, we should rebuild suffix_count properly
        
    # The above approach has an issue with suffix_count updates
    # Let's fix it by building suffix_count properly
    
    # Recompute suffix_count correctly
    suffix_count = {}
    for i in range(n-1, -1, -1):
        suffix_count[a[i]] = suffix_count.get(a[i], 0) + 1
    
    # Reset and redo with proper suffix handling

            third_count = suffix_count.get(third_term, 0)
            
            result += first_count * third_count
        
        # Add current element to prefix_count

        # Update suffix_count - remove current element for the next iteration
        # But we're processing from left to right, so we need to update suffix_count
        # Actually, we can just recalculate suffix_count at each step or precompute it
        
    # We need to fix the suffix_count update
    # Let's rebuild suffix_count correctly in a single pass
    
    # Alternative approach: Build suffix_count once at the beginning
    suffix_count = {}
    for i in range(n-1, -1, -1):
        suffix_count[a[i]] = suffix_count.get(a[i], 0) + 1
    
    result = 0
    prefix_count = {}
    
    # Process each position from left to right
    for j in range(n-1):  # j from 0 to n-2
        # Current element at position j is the middle of our progression
        if a[j] % k == 0:
            b = a[j] // k
            third_term = b * k * k
            
            first_count = prefix_count.get(b, 0)
            third_count = suffix_count.get(third_term, 0)
            
            result += first_count * third_count
        
        # Add current element to prefix_count
        prefix_count[a[j]] = prefix_count.get(a[j], 0) + 1
        
        # Remove current element from suffix_count (for next iteration)
        # We don't need to update suffix_count in real-time since we're only using it for positions after j
        # But we need to ensure suffix_count reflects what's after position j
        
    # The above is still problematic because suffix_count is built at the beginning
    
    # Let's do it differently - build suffix_count such that it only includes elements after current position
    result = 0
    prefix_count = {}
    
    # Precompute suffix counts for each position
    suffix_count = {}
    # Start from the end
    for i in range(n-1, -1, -1):
        # For position i, suffix_count should contain elements from i+1 onwards
        # But we'll build it incrementally
        pass
    
    # Clean approach: build suffix_count at the beginning for the full array
    suffix_count = {}
    for i in range(n-1, -1, -1):
        suffix_count[a[i]] = suffix_count.get(a[i], 0) + 1

    result = 0
    prefix_count = {}
    
    for j in range(n-1):  # j goes from 0 to n-2 (middle element)
        # Check if we can form a valid progression with a[j] as middle
        if a[j] % k == 0:
            b = a[j] // k
            third_term = b * k * k
            
            # Count first terms (b) that occurred before position j
            first_count = prefix_count.get(b, 0)

            # In our suffix_count, we currently have all elements from 0 to n-1
            # We need to exclude elements at position j and positions after j that are not in the correct range
            # Actually, our suffix_count includes everything, so we need to adjust
            
            # Let's rebuild suffix_count to exclude elements at positions we've processed
            # Instead, we'll maintain a running suffix_count
            pass
    
    # Final clean approach:
    result = 0
    prefix_count = {}
    
    # Build suffix_count from right to left
    suffix_count = {}
    for i in range(n-1, -1, -1):
        suffix_count[a[i]] = suffix_count.get(a[i], 0) + 1
    
    # Process each position j as the middle element
    for j in range(n-2):  # j from 0 to n-3 (since we need elements after)
        # Check if a[j] can be b*k
        if a[j] % k == 0:
            b = a[j] // k
            third_term = b * k * k
            
            first_count = prefix_count.get(b, 0)
            third_count = suffix_count.get(third_term, 0)
            
            result += first_count * third_count
        
        # Add current element to prefix_count
        prefix_count[a[j]] = prefix_count.get(a[j], 0) + 1
        
        # Remove current element from suffix_count (since we're processing j, and elements after j should not include a[j])
        # We need to remove a[j] from suffix_count for the next iteration
        if a[j] in suffix_count:
            suffix_count[a[j]] -= 1
            if suffix_count[a[j]] == 0:
                del suffix_count[a[j]]
    
    print(result)
```

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We need to find subsequences of length 3 that form a geometric progression with common ratio `k`
2. A geometric progression with ratio `k` has the form: `b, b*k, b*k²`
3. We need to count how many such triplets (i1, i2, i3) exist where i1 < i2 < i3 and a[i1] * k = a[i2] and a[i2] * k = a[i3]

## Key Insights

1. For three elements to form a geometric progression with ratio k: `a[i1] * k = a[i2]` and `a[i2] * k = a[i3]`
2. This means: `a[i1] * k² = a[i3]`
3. We can iterate through all possible middle elements and count valid pairs
4. For each position `j` (as the middle element), we need to count:
   - How many elements before position `j` have value `a[j] / k`
   - How many elements after position `j` have value `a[j] * k`

## Algorithm Steps

1. For each middle position `j` (from 1 to n-2), check if `a[j]` can be the middle term
2. The first term should be `a[j] / k` and the third term should be `a[j] * k`
3. Count valid pairs by maintaining counts of elements we've seen so far and elements remaining

## Edge Cases

- When `k = 1`: all elements must be equal for a GP
- When `k = 0`: only possible if the middle element is 0
- When `k > 1`: we need to handle division carefully
- When `a[j] / k` is not an integer, skip

## Implementation

```python
n, k = map(int, input().split())
a = list(map(int, input().split()))

count = 0

# For each possible middle element at position j
for j in range(1, n - 1):
    # First element should be a[j] / k, third element should be a[j] * k
    first_val = a[j] / k
    third_val = a[j] * k
    
    # Check if first_val is achievable (integer and exists)
    if first_val == int(first_val):
        first_val = int(first_val)
        
        # Count elements before position j with value first_val
        before_count = 0
        for i in range(j):
            if a[i] == first_val:
                before_count += 1
        
        # Count elements after position j with value third_val
        after_count = 0
        for i in range(j + 1, n):
            if a[i] == third_val:
                after_count += 1
        
        # Add the product of these counts to total
        count += before_count * after_count

print(count)
```

```python
    # All elements must be equal
    count = 0
    for i in range(n):
        if a[i] == a[0]:
            # Count how many equal elements
            pass

    from collections import Counter
    freq = Counter(a)
    
    # For each value, if count >= 3, we can choose any 3 of them
    for val, cnt in freq.items():
        if cnt >= 3:
            count += cnt * (cnt - 1) * (cnt - 2) // 6

        continue
        
    first_val = a[j] // k

    # Count elements before j with value first_val
    before_count = 0
    for i in range(j):
        if a[i] == first_val:
            before_count += 1
    
    # Count elements after j with value third_val
    after_count = 0
```

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We need to find subsequences of length 3 that form a geometric progression with common ratio k
2. A geometric progression with ratio k has the form: b, b*k, b*k²
3. We need to count all valid triplets (i1, i2, i3) where i1 < i2 < i3 and a[i1], a[i2], a[i3] form a geometric progression with ratio k

## Key Insights

1. For three elements to form a geometric progression with ratio k: a[i1], a[i2], a[i3] where a[i2] = a[i1] * k and a[i3] = a[i1] * k²
2. We can iterate through possible middle elements and count how many valid first and third elements exist
3. For each middle element at position i with value v, we need:
   - A previous element with value v/k (if k > 1)
   - A future element with value v*k (if k > 1)
4. Special case: when k = 1, all elements should be equal

## Algorithm

1. Handle the special case when k = 1 (all elements must be equal)
2. For k > 1:
   - For each position i, if a[i] is divisible by k, then a[i] is a middle element in a progression
   - Count how many elements before i have value a[i]/k (potential first elements)
   - Count how many elements after i have value a[i]*k (potential third elements)
   - Add the product of these counts to the result

3. Optimization: Instead of checking every position, we can pre-compute prefix and suffix counts.

## Step-by-step Implementation

```python
n, k = map(int, input().split())
a = list(map(int, input().split()))

if k == 1:
    # All three elements must be equal
    count = 0
    for i in range(n):
        if a[i] == a[0]:  # all elements are the same
            # Number of ways to choose 3 from total count of this value
            total_count = a.count(a[i])
            if total_count >= 3:
                count += total_count * (total_count - 1) * (total_count - 2) // 6
    print(count)
else:
    # k > 1
    result = 0
    
    # Count frequency of each value
    from collections import defaultdict
    freq = defaultdict(int)
    
    # For each position, count how many times we've seen a[i]/k before
    # and how many times we'll see a[i]*k after
    prefix_count = defaultdict(int)
    suffix_count = defaultdict(int)
    
    # Initialize suffix counts (all elements initially)
    for val in a:
        suffix_count[val] += 1
    
    # Process each element as potential middle element
    for i in range(n):
        current_val = a[i]
        
        # Check if current_val is divisible by k
        if current_val % k == 0:
            prev_val = current_val // k  # value of first element
            next_val = current_val * k  # value of third element
            
            # Add contribution from this middle element
            result += prefix_count[prev_val] * suffix_count[next_val]
        
        # Update prefix counts
        prefix_count[current_val] += 1
        
        # Update suffix counts (remove current element)
        suffix_count[current_val] -= 1
        if suffix_count[current_val] == 0:
            del suffix_count[current_val]
    
    print(result)
```

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We need to find the number of subsequences of length 3 that form a geometric progression with common ratio k
2. A geometric progression with ratio k has the form: b, b*k, b*k²
3. We need to count all combinations (i1, i2, i3) where i1 < i2 < i3 and a[i1], a[i2], a[i3] form a geometric progression with ratio k
4. The indices must be strictly increasing

## Key Insights

1. For three elements to form a geometric progression with ratio k: 
   - If we have elements b, b*k, b*k², then for a given middle element b*k, we need to count:
     - How many elements b (before the middle element) such that b*k = middle element
     - How many elements b*k² (after the middle element) such that b*k² = middle element * k

2. We can iterate through each element as the middle element of our triplet, and for each middle element:
   - Calculate what the first element should be: middle_element / k
   - Calculate what the last element should be: middle_element * k
   - Count how many valid first elements appear before the current position
   - Count how many valid last elements appear after the current position
   - Multiply these counts to get the number of valid triplets with this middle element

3. Special cases:
   - When k = 1: all elements are equal, so we need to count combinations of three identical elements
   - When k = 0: we need to be careful as division by zero occurs

## Step-by-step Solution

1. Read input
2. Handle special case when k = 1 (all elements are equal)
3. For each position as the middle element:
   - Calculate the required first and last elements
   - Count valid first elements before this position
   - Count valid last elements after this position
   - Add the product to answer
4. Return answer

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We need to find the number of subsequences of length 3 that form a geometric progression with common ratio k
2. A geometric progression with ratio k has the form: b, b*k, b*k²
3. We need to count triplets (i, j, l) where i < j < l and a[i], a[j], a[l] form a geometric progression with ratio k

## Key Insights

1. For three elements to form a geometric progression with ratio k: if we have elements at positions i, j, l, then:
   - a[j] = a[i] * k
   - a[l] = a[i] * k²
   
2. We can reframe this: for each possible middle element (at position j), we need to count:
   - How many elements before j have value = a[j] / k
   - How many elements after j have value = a[j] * k

3. Then the total count for middle element j would be: (count of valid first element) × (count of valid third element)

## Edge Cases to Consider

1. When k = 1: all elements must be equal for GP
2. When k ≠ 1: we need to handle division properly
3. When a[j] / k is not an integer (or when a[j] * k is not achievable)
4. Negative numbers: need to be careful with division

## Step-by-Step Solution

1. For each possible middle position j (from 1 to n-2, 0-indexed):
   - Check if a[j] is divisible by k (when k > 1)
   - If k = 1, we just need to find pairs of equal elements before and after
   - Otherwise, find how many elements before j equal a[j] / k
   - Find how many elements after j equal a[j] * k
   - Add the product to our answer

2. We can optimize by precomputing prefix and suffix arrays to avoid repeated counting.

## Implementation

```python
n, k = map(int, input().split())
a = list(map(int, input().split()))

count = 0

# For each possible middle element at position j
for j in range(1, n - 1):
    # For the sequence to be geometric with ratio k:
    # We need a[j] = first_element * k, and a[j] * k = third_element
    
    # First element should be a[j] / k
    # Third element should be a[j] * k
    
    # Special case: k = 1
    if k == 1:
        # All elements must be equal
        first_val = a[j]
        # Count elements before j that equal first_val
        before_count = sum(1 for i in range(j) if a[i] == first_val)
        # Count elements after j that equal first_val
        after_count = sum(1 for i in range(j + 1, n) if a[i] == first_val)
        count += before_count * after_count
    else:
        # k != 1
        # Check if a[j] is divisible by k
        if a[j] % k == 0:
            first_val = a[j] // k
            third_val = a[j] * k
            
            # Count elements before j that equal first_val
            before_count = sum(1 for i in range(j) if a[i] == first_val)
            # Count elements after j that equal third_val
            after_count = sum(1 for i in range(j + 1, n) if a[i] == third_val)
            count += before_count * after_count
        # If a[j] is not divisible by k, we can't form a valid geometric progression

print(count)
```

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Solve the following coding problem using the programming language python:

Polycarp loves geometric progressions very much. Since he was only three years old, he loves only the progressions of length three. He also has a favorite integer k and a sequence a, consisting of n integers.

He wants to know how many subsequences of length three can be selected from a, so that they form a geometric progression with common ratio k.

A subsequence of length three is a combination of three such indexes i1, i2, i3, that 1 ≤ i1 < i2 < i3 ≤ n. That is, a subsequence of length three are such groups of three elements that are not necessarily consecutive in the sequence, but their indexes are strictly increasing.

A geometric progression with common ratio k is a sequence of numbers of the form b·k0, b·k1, ..., b·kr - 1.

Polycarp is only three years old, so he can not calculate this number himself. Help him to do it.

Input

The first line of the input contains two integers, n and k (1 ≤ n, k ≤ 2·105), showing how many numbers Polycarp's sequence has and his favorite number.

The second line contains n integers a1, a2, ..., an ( - 109 ≤ ai ≤ 109) — elements of the sequence.

Output

Output a single number — the number of ways to choose a subsequence of length three, such that it forms a geometric progression with a common ratio k.

Examples

Input

5 2
1 1 2 2 4


Output

4

Input

3 1
1 1 1


Output

1

Input

10 3
1 2 6 2 3 6 9 18 3 9


Output

6

Note

In the first sample test the answer is four, as any of the two 1s can be chosen as the first element, the second element can be any of the 2s, and the third element of the subsequence must be equal to 4.

The input will be given via stdin and the output should be printed to stdout by your code.

Now solve the problem by providing the code.### Format: Read the inputs from stdin solve the problem and write the answer to stdout (do not directly test on the sample inputs). Enclose your code within delimiters as follows. Ensure that when the python program runs, it reads the inputs, runs the algorithm and writes output to STDOUT.
```python
# YOUR CODE HERE
```

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We need to find the number of subsequences of length 3 that form a geometric progression with common ratio k.
2. A geometric progression with ratio k has the form: b, b*k, b*k²
3. We need to count all triplets (i, j, m) where i < j < m and a[i] = b, a[j] = b*k, a[m] = b*k² for some value b.

## Key Insights

1. For three elements to form a geometric progression with ratio k, if we have elements at positions i < j < m, then:
   - a[i] * k² = a[m] and a[i] * k = a[j]
   
2. We can iterate through all possible middle elements (at position j), and for each middle element, count how many valid first elements and third elements exist.

3. For a middle element a[j] = x, we need:
   - First element: value = x/k (if k ≠ 1, or any value if k = 1)
   - Third element: value = x*k (if k ≠ 1, or any value if k = 1)

4. Special case: when k = 1, all elements are equal, so any three equal elements form a GP with ratio 1.

## Algorithm

1. Handle the special case when k = 1: we just need to count combinations of 3 from the total frequency of each value.

2. For k ≠ 1:
   - For each position j (as the middle element), find how many values equal to a[j]/k have appeared before (at positions < j)
   - Find how many values equal to a[j]*k appear after (at positions > j)
   - Multiply these counts for each position j

3. To efficiently count elements before and after, we can precompute prefix sums and suffix sums.

## Step-by-Step Solution

```python
def solve():
    n, k = map(int, input().split())
    a = list(map(int, input().split()))
    
    if k == 1:
        # When k = 1, any three equal elements form a GP with ratio 1
        # Count frequency of each element
        freq = {}
        for num in a:
            freq[num] = freq.get(num, 0) + 1
        
        total = 0
        for count in freq.values():
            if count >= 3:
                total += count * (count - 1) * (count - 2) // 6
        return total
    
    # For k != 1
    # We'll count for each middle position j
    # How many valid first elements (a[i] = a[j]/k) with i < j
    # How many valid third elements (a[m] = a[j]*k) with m > j
    
    # First, create a map to store the prefix count of each value
    prefix_count = {}
    
    # Suffix count will be computed from the end
    suffix_count = {}
    
    # Count frequency from right to left
    for i in range(n - 1, -1, -1):
        val = a[i]
        suffix_count[val] = suffix_count.get(val, 0) + 1
    
    total_ways = 0
    
    # Process each position as the middle element
    for j in range(n):
        current_val = a[j]
        
        # Check if we can have a valid first element (current_val / k)
        first_val = current_val / k
        
        # Check if first_val is an integer
        if first_val != int(first_val):
            # Can't form GP with this middle element
            pass
        else:
            first_val_int = int(first_val)
            # Count how many first elements we've seen before position j
            prefix_count[first_val_int] = prefix_count.get(first_val_int, 0) + 1
            
            # Check if we can have a valid third element (current_val * k)
            third_val = current_val * k
            
            # Count how many third elements we'll see after position j
            suffix_count[third_val] = suffix_count.get(third_val, 0) - 1
            
            # We need to update prefix_count as we go
            # Actually, let me restructure - we need to process each j and count before and after
            
    # Let me rework this with a cleaner approach
    
    # Reset suffix_count
    suffix_count = {}
    for i in range(n - 1, -1, -1):
        suffix_count[a[i]] = suffix_count.get(a[i], 0) + 1
    
    # Process from left to right
    prefix_count = {}
    total_ways = 0
    
    for j in range(n):
        current_val = a[j]
        
        # Check if current_val is divisible by k
        if current_val % k != 0:
            # Can't form GP with this as middle element
            pass
        else:
            first_val = current_val // k
            
            # Count how many times first_val appears before j
            first_count = prefix_count.get(first_val, 0)
            
            # Count how many times third_val appears after j
            third_val = current_val * k
            third_count = suffix_count.get(third_val, 0)
            
            total_ways += first_count * third_count
        
        # Add current value to prefix_count for future positions
        prefix_count[current_val] = prefix_count.get(current_val, 0) + 1
        
        # Remove current value from suffix_count (since we've processed it)
        suffix_count[current_val] = suffix_count.get(current_val, 0) - 1
        if suffix_count[current_val] == 0:
            del suffix_count[current_val]
    
    return total_ways

print(solve())
```

```python
    from collections import Counter
    freq = Counter(a)
    total = 0
    for count in freq.values():
        if count >= 3:
            total += count * (count - 1) * (count - 2) // 6
    print(total)

    # How many valid third elements (a[m] = a[j]*k) with m > j
    
    # First, create suffix count - how many times each value appears after position j

    for i in range(n - 1, -1, -1):
        val = a[i]
        suffix_count[val] = suffix_count.get(val, 0) + 1
    
    total_ways = 0
    prefix_count = {}
    
    for j in range(n):
        current_val = a[j]

        if current_val % k != 0:
            # Skip this position
            pass
        else:
            first_val = current_val // k
            third_val = current_val * k
```
